.claude/rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:442:## Mechanical-mirror exception — T1 SKIP for downstream-propagation ships (n=2 self-observed + n=1 user-triggered codification — same-session 2026-05-02)
.claude/rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:444:T1 may be skipped only when the edit is pure downstream-propagation of a **settled upstream authority** to a sister/parallel surface (rule-layer ↔ skill-layer ↔ canonical-authority-surface). **Settled** means either (a) the upstream concept has terminal T1 APPROVE, or NEEDS-REVISION with all prescribed edits integrated, cited with `[VERIFIED via <verdict path>]` plus commit SHA, or (b) an explicit user-trigger directive is being copied as recorded authority with no interpretation. REJECT, INCONCLUSIVE, UNKNOWN, timeout-with-red-flags, or NEEDS-REVISION-with-open-prescriptions fail closed to fresh T1. This is a scoped mechanical-mirror exception, not a generic "looks mechanical" exception. T2 commit-time hook (`.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t2_pre_commit_gate.py:424` PreToolUse `Bash(git commit *)` per `.claude/rules/layered-gates-architecture.md` §5 Layer 2) is the cross-model verification net.

.claude/rules\citation-discipline.md:35:## Rule: Every new pattern MUST cite its source at file:line depth
.claude/rules\citation-discipline.md:42:6. **TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE class for inherited patterns** — when a SOTA pattern is derived from a named author's published work (book / peer-reviewed paper / conference talk), cite verbatim-quote + author-name + publication inline. The named-author cite is TIER-1 primary-source for the PATTERN (not the implementing repo); use alongside repo file:line cites for full provenance. Exemplar: `Z:/repos/deps/mattpocock/skills/README.md` (48,857★, MIT, [VERIFIED 2026-04-30 PM via curl raw GitHub]) quotes Pragmatic Programmer (Thomas & Hunt) for "rate of feedback is your speed limit" (failure-mode #3 framing), Domain-Driven-Design (Eric Evans) for ubiquitous-language pattern (#2 framing), and Extreme Programming Explained (Kent Beck) for "invest in design every day" (#4 framing). Per `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota-installed/memory/reference_mattpocock_skills_pattern_extract_2026_04_30.md` for the full mining trace + adoption-candidate ranking. **TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE PROVISIONAL-EXCEPTION** *(round-3 framing per T3 round-2 conf=0.88 ESCALATING finding on commit `d4df352`; supersedes round-1 "full cardinal-rule-1 satisfaction" framing — physical-only sources do NOT fully satisfy committed-tree inspectability)*: physical-only printed-book/journal/conference-publication primary anchor (ISBN + page + verbatim-quote) is a PROVISIONAL-EXCEPTION to cardinal-rule-1 (CLAUDE.md L7), NOT full satisfaction. Cite class downgraded to TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL with REQUIRED metadata: (a) ISBN/DOI + page + verbatim-quote inline; (b) verifier-identity disclosure (who-verified-when, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-04 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`); (c) explicit `non-automatable: true` marker so review hooks know to defer to human sign-off; (d) **HARD-GATE ELIGIBILITY TEST** *(Round-3 amendment a per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "MUST prefer" soft preference framing — preference is not gateable; eligibility is)*: PROVISIONAL admission is admissible ONLY when DOI landing page + accessible PDF / publisher excerpt / archived scan (license-permitting) was SEARCHED AND UNAVAILABLE. The commit body MUST document the search (which DOI/publisher/archive was queried + return shape — e.g., "searched ACM DOI 10.1145/<doi> 2026-05-05 — paywall-only, no excerpt found; searched Internet Archive — no scan; searched publisher excerpt page — none"). Mere preference is INSUFFICIENT — if any auditable surrogate exists (DOI/PDF/excerpt/scan), use it as the normative source instead of the physical-only book cite. e.g., ACM DOI `https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/<doi>` for ACM papers IS auditable when paywall-free. Secondary aggregator URLs (Wikipedia, `https://pragprog.com/tips/`, `https://martinfowler.com/bliki/<topic>.html`, `c2.com/cgi/wiki`) are VERIFICATION PROXIES only — they help re-verify the primary quote without owning the book physically, but are NOT cited as normative source. **Independent named-reviewer sign-off mandate** *(Round-3 amendment b per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "Lower-confidence reviewer-sign-off mandate" sole-orchestrator framing — sole-orchestrator verification is no longer admissible)*: rule edits citing TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL MUST cite a SECOND verifier-identity BEYOND the orchestrator who replayed the verbatim quote against the source — commit body MUST embed BOTH verifier-identity-1 (the orchestrator, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`) AND verifier-identity-2 (the independent named reviewer, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by <human-name> against same book + same page + same quote]`) with explicit `[VERIFIED yyyy-mm-dd by <name> against <source>]` markers for each. The 2-verifier requirement closes the "self-attestation only" gap that round-2 left open. **`non-automatable: true` operator-discipline mandate (manual-only; no hook enforcement YET)** *(Round-4 amendment per T3 round-4 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 SAME-CLASS finding on commit `d07ac71`; supersedes Round-3 amendment c "BLOCKING in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape" claim that was prose-only without hook implementation — codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE caught the policy/enforcement drift: rule text claimed automated BLOCKING but no T3/T4/T6 hook, verdict parser, schema, or test changed. Per closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md §Outcome A monotone-decline path, downgraded prose closes the drift while the enforcement-hook ship is queued separately)*: when a TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL cite is shipped, the operator MUST: (i) embed `non-automatable: true` marker inline; (ii) embed explicit "no automated enforcement, human gate only" disclosure in commit body acknowledging that automated re-verification is impossible AND naming both verifier-identities (per amendment b); (iii) accept residual provenance gap explicitly. The discipline is OPERATOR-MANUAL — NO T3/T4/T6 hook currently parses `non-automatable: true` as BLOCKING. Promotion to mechanical BLOCKING is FORWARD-REF for a separate enforcement-hook ship: `tools/non_automatable_blocking_audit.py` parsing committed rule edits + emitting BLOCKING status in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape + regression tests at `tests/test_non_automatable_blocking_audit.py`. Do NOT claim BLOCKING semantic in commit bodies until that ship lands. Forward-only convention codified Wave 18 PSW arc — Bundle B Round-2 commit `d6acfb1`, Bundle B Round-3 commit `217430f`, karpathy 5-author commit `0fe6282`, citation-discipline Round-2 DEMOTE commit `1b87a6e`, citation-discipline Round-3 hard-gate commit `d07ac71`, this Round-4 prose-downgrade commit closes the architectural concern per `reference_wave18_psw_arc_close_4ship_2026_05_04.md` + Outcome A monotone-decline path per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` (R1 conf=0.86 → R2 conf=0.88 ESCALATING → R3 conf=0.82 DECLINING → R4 conf=0.82 SAME-CLASS DECLINING-STABLE; severity stable medium; severity-gate satisfied via concrete-verification mechanism per amendments a+b + prose-downgrade per amendment c; arc converging within Round 5 ceiling).

.claude/rules\fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md:66:Pool-funneling per CLAUDE.local.md ENV (f) means simultaneous parallel agents drain Sonnet pool exactly when handoff fires. **For ADOPT-class verdicts**: lost adversarial-review surface BLOCKS the ship until either (a) reviewer re-dispatched and returns verdict, OR (b) explicitly equivalent cross-model review (codex T1 deep-review-exec) completes. **Mia pre-apply is a prescription-validity check ONLY** — does NOT satisfy or replace the adversarial-review gate for ADOPT-class verdicts (per T3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.88 iter-2 escalation on commit `2e7deae`). **STAND-IN-NOTICE mandate** (Wave 48 Agent A F-1 conf=0.91 on commit `5e0bf2b`): if orchestrator-side Mia substitutes for re-dispatch, commit body MUST include `gpt5-reviewer LOST (FM-17.b pool-depletion); adversarial-review surface substituted via orchestrator Mia pre-apply; cross-model gate NOT satisfied for this ship; T1 re-review queued.` per `cross-model-consensus.md §Env-funneled subagent stand-in disclosure mandate`. Disclosure alone does NOT satisfy the gate.
.claude/rules\fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md:72:**.c.i candidate root causes** (Wave 26 Agent A diagnosis): (1) cwd/slug-hash mismatch — companion `state.json` writes to `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}/state/<slug>-<sha256(cwd).slice(0,16)>/` per `state.mjs:8-52 @ HEAD 807e03ac`, dispatch-cwd may differ from probe-cwd (MOST CONSISTENT for Wave 25 task-mot7qnh5-azhcys); (2) spawn-before-write TOCTOU at `codex-companion.mjs:654-680`; (3) Windows ACL on `.codex/sessions`; (4) CODEX_HOME env drift; (5) PowerShell vs Bash subprocess handle inheritance. **.c.ii root cause**: `CertOpenCurrentUserStore` denied in worktree process even though `CODEX_HOME` auth files present. Per `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern B` HNF: examine bg job stderr/tee files for partial trace; if mineable, integrate as Sonnet stand-in BLOCKED-PROVISIONAL. **Wave 26 Lesson 2 correction**: foreground+tee fixes .c.i but NOT .c.ii; per `port-note-discipline.md §6` correction is FORWARD-ONLY.
.claude/rules\fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md:75:Sonnet wrapper's stream-watchdog 600s timeout is shorter than typical foreground `codex exec -p deep-review-exec` xhigh reasoning duration (empirical 5-15min per `cross-model-consensus.md §Profile selection rule`). Watchdog fires before codex emits next stream chunk; wrapper has no recovery primitive. **Cascade BLOCK gate (.d + .c.ii)** (Wave 48 Agent A F-3 conf=0.82): if foreground+tee from MAIN orchestrator session ALSO fails with cert-store ACL signature, STOP all retries; emit `VERDICT: BLOCK — BRIDGE-MODE and main-session foreground+tee both unavailable (FM-17.d + FM-17.c.ii cascade); cross-model gate suspended; no ship until Windows cert-store ACL remediated`. Pressure-driven shortcut to defer-queue WITHOUT BLOCK is anti-pattern. Sister to .c.i recovery (foreground+tee) and `cross-model-consensus.md §Env-funneled subagent stand-in disclosure mandate` STAND-IN-NOTICE — when .d fires and operator pivots to foreground+tee, verdict origin remains REAL GPT-5.5 (cross-model gate satisfied).
.claude/rules\fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md:78:CC-runtime autocompact mechanism: when single tool output exceeds context-window threshold, autocompact fires; if agent's brief re-issues same large-content tool call, post-compact context refills immediately and triggers autocompact again. After 3 consecutive thrash-cycles, CC-runtime self-aborts. Differs from .d in failure surface: .d is Sonnet-wrapper-watchdog (600s gap on codex-CLI stream); .e is CC-runtime-autocompact (context refilled 3 turns post-compact, 3 times). For LOST verdict: trace-mining is N/A (no partial output); HONEST-NON-FINDING per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories`. Same-fire n=2 in Wave 112 Ship A1 dispatch (Agents B `a9b7a29764e29782f` + C `a3c48b6aed8ece998` both BRIDGE-MODE codex-rescue with same multi-source ecosystem probe scope) — diagnostic discriminator vs .d is literal notification text ("stream watchdog" vs "Autocompact is thrashing").

.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:107:## Timeout + failover policy
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:121:> **⚠️ CAUTION — Option (b) `/api/generate` endpoint is UNCERTIFIED.** Neither the `/api/generate` endpoint nor the `qwen3.6:judge` model's verdict-shape compatibility has been smoke-tested for cross-model T1 verdict-composition. **First-use of option (b) MUST probe before trusting verdicts**: send a minimal payload with `"stream": false` set explicitly (e.g., `{"model": "qwen3.6:judge", "prompt": "APPROVE, NEEDS-REVISION, or REJECT?", "stream": false}` — without `"stream": false`, Ollama `/api/generate` defaults to NDJSON streaming and the JSON-shape parse below will false-negative even when endpoint is healthy) + verify `{"response": "..."}` JSON shape returns within 60s + verify model emits APPROVE / NEEDS-REVISION / REJECT structured output as expected by `cross-model-consensus.md §Verdict shapes`. If smoke-probe fails, fall back to option (a) deferred-queue rather than trust an uncertified verdict.

.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:217:## Risk-stratified verification gating
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:219:# Reference: `https://resources.anthropic.com/2026-agentic-coding-trends-report` (TIER-1 Anthropic; full PDF at `https://resources.anthropic.com/hubfs/2026%20Agentic%20Coding%20Trends%20Report.pdf`) [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 via Wave-6 Agent M perplexity_research [17][31][48]] — Anthropic 2026 Agentic Coding Trends Report identifies risk-stratified gating as the SOTA pattern for AI-agent code change verification: low-risk = full-agent-delegate + post-commit review; medium-risk = pre-commit human review (agent implements, human approves); high-risk = design review BEFORE agent implements.
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:220:# Reference: Wave-6 Agent M Round-4 task design at `tmp/wave6-agent-m-sota-git-practice-2026-05-03.md:165-196` (ADOPT-NOW conf=0.92; cohort attribution C5 named-author Anthropic; Axis 1+2+3 PASS via STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS predicate satisfied — Anthropic-org-as-named-T2-equivalent maintainer).
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:222:Beyond the **finding-severity** taxonomy at §"Severity taxonomy mapping" above (which classifies what codex found AFTER work landed), the **task risk-class** axis classifies the change BEFORE work begins. Both axes are orthogonal — severity describes findings (P0/P1/P2/P3); risk-class describes tasks (low/medium/high). Both inform the T0-T7 lifecycle gate selection.
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:228:| **Low-risk** | Reversible operations on isolated surfaces (tests / docs / non-cardinal-rule edits / bounded scripts ≤200 LOC where convergence is well-understood); single-axis-change with clear primary-source backing | Full-agent-delegate + **post-commit review only** (T3 PostToolUse `Bash(git commit *)`) | T1 SKIP per §"Mechanical-mirror exception" at `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md` IF predicates met; else light T1 + T3 verify |
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:239:2. **Select gate-set per risk-class** (per §The 3-tier risk taxonomy table above)
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:251:- `severity-taxonomy-mapping` §above — orthogonal axis (severity describes findings AFTER; risk-class describes tasks BEFORE)
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:252:- `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Mechanical-mirror exception` — operationalizes T1 SKIP for the low-risk + settled-source predicate combination
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:253:- `layered-gates-architecture.md §The 5 layers` — architectural authority for the gate layers each risk-class invokes
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:254:- `agent-harness-fit-verification.md §The 7 sub-classes` — Probe DAG that high-risk T0 candidate-list challenge invokes before T1 fires
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:255:- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` — UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY-CLAIM classification interacts with risk-class (CATEGORY-CLAIM ships often classify as low-risk; OPERATIONAL-CLAIM with new mechanics often medium-risk; UPSTREAM-CLAIM for new cited-dep adoption often high-risk)

.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:267:## Evidence marker discipline (evidence-policy integration)
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:278:**MUST-USE `[VERIFIED via <path>]` convention extension** (F-C-02 forward-only codification iter-107): every commit body citing a T1/T2/T3/T4/T6 verdict with `confidence ≥ 0.85` (or any conf=N claim) MUST embed inline the verifying file path in `[VERIFIED via <path>]` form — e.g. `T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.90 [VERIFIED via .claude/state/codex_consult_iter106_fm02_owner_propagation_OUT.txt]`. This closes the OPERATIONAL-CLAIM ambiguity per `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator`: without the path-anchored `[VERIFIED via <path>]` marker, readers cannot distinguish OPERATIONAL-CLAIM (verdict file actually contains the cited verdict) from CATEGORY-CLAIM (verdict file exists at the path but content unverified) without re-probing. **Forward-only application per `.claude/rules/port-note-discipline.md §6` anti-pattern "Do not rewrite historical commit bodies/snapshots to fix retroactive findings"** — historical iter-92→iter-101 commit bodies (8 of 9 commits in arc 2026-05-02 missing this marker per Wave 8 Agent C F-C-02 P2 finding at task `a89f4e9f163723385`) remain unmodified. iter-104 commit `9a22d27` exemplifies correct usage; iter-105 `011d885` + iter-106 `5747e10` carry forward; this rule's codification governs iter-107+ forward.
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:280:## Source-cite discipline for consult prompts
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:304:## Anti-patterns
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:313:- **Cite commit hashes in stable rule §Related cross-refs** — commit hashes belong in commit messages and provenance, not in cross-rule edges (per iter-8 codex finding).
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:314:- **Trust first T1 verdict hit instead of file END** — codex deep-review-exec outputs span 1k-10k lines and routinely echo the `VERDICT: APPROVE | NEEDS-REVISION | REJECT` template near the start before the actual investigation, then emit the real verdict at EOF. Discipline: `wc -l <file>` first; then `grep -nE "VERDICT:|^F-[0-9]|conf=|confidence|APPROVE|NEEDS-REVISION|REJECT|severity (P[0-3])" <file> | tail -20` (force last hits, not first); then `tail -200 <file>` and confirm the final verdict block is at EOF. Genuine-truncation branch: only call `INCONCLUSIVE-INCOMPLETE` when the last grep hits are prompt-echo / early-investigation AND `tail -200` ends mid-investigation with no final verdict — high line count alone does NOT imply completion. Empirically refuted on iter-17/18/20 completed T1 verdict files (n=3 verdicts at L4457/L10073/L1610 + EOF) plus iter-19 separate skipped-T1 propagation case — total n=4 affected commit-body claims, n=3 verified completed outputs. Failure class: `synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Reporting categories OVER. Operational home: this rule's verdict-reading + fail-closed protocol. Provenance: `feedback_codex_t1_verdict_reading_discipline.md` + iter-21 fix-forward.

VERIFY corollaries 1+2 are pre-edit and post-edit observability that bookend this rule's during-edit consensus.
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:321:- `.claude/rules/closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` — re-fire arc disposition: **Outcome A ACCEPT-WITH-DOC** / **Outcome B REVERT-AND-REMOVE** / **Outcome C MANUAL-OVERRIDE**. First-round T1 verdict disposition is distinct (see §Verdict shapes); closed-loop outcomes only invoke on same-surface re-fire.
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:328:## Eval-case mandate (Phase 1 of Task #88 verdict→eval gap closure)
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:338:Bare prose-only verdicts without one of (1) or (2) are NON-COMPLIANT. The eval-case mandate is the operational form of "compounding learning across fires" that `karpathy-adapted.md` §5 describes — without replayable cases, the same class of miss can recur silently.
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:340:**REJECT verdicts**: still file an eval case if a state-flip is the fix (e.g. plan §2 row → RETIRE/REJECT-status); the case verifies the flip happened. If the REJECT is a no-ship outcome with no state to verify, record the reason in the wiki Run Log instead.

.claude/rules\deprecation-discipline.md:9:# Reference: constituents=[TIER-1-DIRECT @ Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/addy-agent-skills/skills/deprecation-and-migration/SKILL.md:10-12 @742dca5, TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION @ claude-sota-installed adaptation]; effective_tier=TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION per citation-discipline.md rule #8 [VERIFIED 2026-05-08 via Wave 82m-B sota-researcher Top-3 CITE-IMPORT #2 + Wave 82x codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION fix-forward F-001 cite-class reframe] — addyosmani/agent-skills (33,500 stars MIT, named-author Addy Osmani / Google Chrome team). Upstream verbatim at SKILL.md:10-12: "Code is a liability, not an asset. Every line of code has ongoing maintenance cost — bugs to fix, dependencies to update, security patches to apply, and new engineers to onboard. Deprecation is the discipline of removing code that no longer earns its keep, and migration is the process of moving users safely from the old to the new."

.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:43:# TIER-3 local codification provenance only: this rule was promoted from `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Pre-apply Mia rule on agent ship-prescriptions` sub-section (Wave 14 codification commit `10d9455` 2026-05-04) to standalone rule after n=8 cumulative dogfood.
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:47:The pattern: **AGENT reads brief context but DOES NOT probe runtime state → produces CATEGORY-CLAIM that LOOKS LIKE OPERATIONAL-CLAIM** (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator`). Agents systematically conflate "should be wired" with "is wired", "should be 80" with "is 80", "is unwired" with "is wired", without checking actual state via `git log` / `Grep` / `Glob`. This rule converts post-hoc OVER detection (existing `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` shape) into PRE-emptive verification (this rule's shape) BEFORE any Edit fires.
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:49:## When this rule applies
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:64:## How to apply (BEFORE writing any Edit)
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:68:1. **Decompose the gap-claim** into testable sub-claims (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Within-cycle revision discipline (axis-9)`)
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:69:2. **Pick the cheapest probe per sub-claim** (the §Subclaim-type discriminator informs probe-type selection):
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:75:   - SHAPE-CLAIM (settings.json hook entry / .mcp.json server / hook JSONL payload) → `Grep` + schema-validate per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Output-form verification modifier`
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:77:4. **Classify a refuted gap-prescription as OVER and drop it.** Reserve HONEST-NON-FINDING for an agent/operator report that explicitly probed an assumed target and found no such target (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` OVER vs HNF distinction). Do NOT silently apply the prescription; document the refutation in commit body
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:78:5. **Apply only the verified-surviving prescriptions** in the single atomic Pattern A fix-forward commit per `.claude/rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern A` — this rule is an admissibility filter BEFORE Pattern A's atomic-apply step (first drop OVER prescriptions, then apply all surviving verified prescriptions atomically)

.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:150:## Why this works
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:158:## Anti-patterns
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:162:- **Cite the agent's verdict file as authority for the prescription** — per `cross-model-consensus.md §Source-cite discipline for consult prompts`: agent verdicts (`.claude/state/codex_consult_*_OUT.txt` + agent inline returns) are TIER-3 evidence trails, NOT TIER-1 sources. For local operational claims, the checked file/log/git history is the controlling operational evidence; the agent's prescription is a hypothesis pending probe-confirmation, not a verified fact.
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:164:- **Conflate OVER with HONEST-NON-FINDING** — these are distinct `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories`: OVER = report claims more than git shows (refuted prescription class); HNF = report says "I probed for X, found nothing" (agent did the probe). A refuted gap-prescription where I probed and found the gap closed = OVER, NOT HNF (the agent didn't probe; I did).
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:167:- **PATH-only probe for INSTALL-class prescriptions** — refuted by Wave 112 Ship A1+2CC archeology n=29→36 (5 OVER catches in single ship). `command -v <cli>` only checks PATH-availability — MISSES alternate install paths (`.local/bin/`, `.cargo/bin/`, `~/go/bin/`, npm-global, WinGet, uv tool dirs). Cost: 115 MB shadow installs + 3 follow-up ships to operationally close. Use the fortified multi-channel probe per §Alternate-install-path probe discipline above.
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:169:## Sister-rule integration
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:171:- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` — post-hoc OVER detection on agent returns; this rule is the PRE-emptive specialization
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:172:- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` — UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY-CLAIM classification IS the type-system for the Mia-rule probe selection (CATEGORY → cheap-grep; OPERATIONAL → runtime-probe; UPSTREAM → re-WebFetch)
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:173:- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Output-form verification modifier (SHAPE-CLAIM)` — when the prescription targets a structured-output surface (settings.json hook entry / .mcp.json / hook JSONL), use the SHAPE-CLAIM probe path (compile/schema-validate) as the cheapest probe
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:174:- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Within-cycle revision discipline (axis-9)` — decompose-before-flip applies recursively to agent prescriptions

.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:176:- `cross-model-consensus.md §Verdict report shape (synthesis-layer-verify integration)` — OVER detection on codex output; this rule generalizes to all agent classes (architect / sota-researcher / Explore / etc.)
.claude/rules\mia-pre-apply.md:177:- `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern A` — this rule is an admissibility filter before Pattern A: first drop/refute OVER prescriptions, then apply all surviving verified prescriptions in the single atomic Pattern A fix-forward commit. The rule does NOT silently weaken Pattern A's atomic-apply requirement; it tightens the input set BEFORE Pattern A applies

.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:27:# Parallel session worktree isolation — outer-layer Cherny pattern
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:29:## The rule
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:31:When TWO OR MORE `claude` CLI sessions operate concurrently against the same checkout, EACH session MUST start with `claude --worktree` (or `-w`) so each session gets its own isolated working tree under `.claude/worktrees/`. The launcher `tools/eee.ps1` forwards `--worktree`/`-w` per `tools/eee.ps1:--worktree forwarding block` symbol-anchor (per `port-note-discipline.md §2 Discipline 1`) (shipped via codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.88 gap-resolution arc 2026-05-02 commit `f3c1e57`) — mandate is now hard MUST, no longer SHOULD-WHEN-AVAILABLE. Without this isolation, one session's `git rm` + `git add` + `git commit` sequence can interleave with another session's pending stage operations, producing audit-trail mismatches where commit content does not match commit message.
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:33:This rule applies to the OUTER (CLI launch) layer. The INNER (agent-spawn) layer is already covered: all 8 sss agents declare `isolation: worktree` per `^isolation:` grep across `.claude/agents/` [VERIFIED 2026-05-04 via Wave 18 architect audit grep returned 8/8]; symbol-anchor avoids stale-count drift per `port-note-discipline.md §1 Discipline 1`. Agent-level isolation handles `Agent()` tool spawns; outer-level isolation handles separate `claude` invocations.
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:35:## Why
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:49:## How to apply
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:54:# Wrong (parallel sessions same checkout)
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:58:# Right (each session in its own worktree)
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:64:Ask Claude: "move this work to a worktree". CC will create one and migrate the in-flight changes via `git worktree add` per code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference §worktree.
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:72:## Sub-classes (cumulative iter-99 codification — META-router enriched at `named-failure-modes.md` FM-02 row)
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:80:**Recovery** (per FM-02 META-router row #1 + #3 + cardinal-rule reporting): narrow `git add -- <my-file>` BEFORE T2 fires (row #1) + close via single-shell `git add -- <my-file> && git commit -o <my-file> -F <msg>` chain after the T2 check (row #3 atomic-batch per `audit-action-loop.md` step 4) + report contamination per cardinal-rule 7 (CLAUDE.md §Hard Rules, bullet "REPORT errors before routing around them") rather than silently filtering it.
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:92:**Recovery** (per FM-02 META-router row #4): accept the absorption (do NOT rewrite history per `port-note-discipline.md §6` anti-pattern "Do not rewrite historical commit bodies"); document via supplementary memory + audit-trail correction in subsequent commit body when feasible.
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:96:Each session uses `claude --worktree` for full filesystem isolation (Layer 0 of `layered-gates-architecture.md` §3 worktree isolation prerequisite). Filesystem-level isolation prevents all 3 sub-classes structurally; the narrow-`--only` recovery patterns above are stop-gap defenses until full Layer-0 isolation is universal across operator launch paths.
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:102:**Verification query** (per `port-note-discipline.md §5 Discipline 4` n-counter audit format): `rg -n "FM-02 staging contamination|bundle6-skills-audit|Verdict" .claude/state/codex_t2_iter105_a2a_protocol_catalog_row_OUT.txt` returns explicit hits at L375 (P1 HIGH FM-02 staging contamination present) + L393 (Q6 VERIFIED contamination scope across 5 parallel-session staged + modified + untracked files) + L398 (NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.94 commit-readiness verdict) + L403 (P1 staged unrelated deletion blocks clean commit readiness — re-stated finding from L375). .claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:104:## Mandatory mechanical collision-guard (Round-2 fix-forward per T3 round-1 NEEDS-ATTENTION)
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:110:1. **Persist `agent_id` + `agent_type` when present in hook input** (per `audit-action-loop.md §Hook telemetry contract`). For main-session writes where `agent_id` is absent (None / null), persist `null` explicitly so consumers can distinguish "main session" from "absent attribution". This is **attribution**, NOT collision prevention.
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:119:Two parallel sessions with different `agent_id` values STILL race on the same shared state path under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude` env-pinning per §Anti-patterns above. The `agent_id` field disambiguates JSONL records POST-hoc (which agent wrote which entry); it does NOT prevent two writers from racing on the same file path. Collision prevention REQUIRES the boundary in step 2 of §The mandate above.
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:127:Operator discipline (named-topic uniqueness) per §Anti-patterns "Topic-named verdict file write-collision" COMPLEMENTS but does NOT replace the mechanical boundary above. Defense-in-depth: mechanical boundary (mandatory) + operator discipline (advisory).
.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:131:Per `port-note-discipline.md §3 Discipline 2` FORWARD-REF retirement: one previously-FORWARD-REF guard ELEVATES to enforceable mandate this round-2 fix-forward. The chosen guard: `_atomic_jsonl_append.py` shared module that wraps every JSONL append with the boundary in step 2 of §The mandate above (atomic temp-rename + `os.replace` for full-file writes; `O_APPEND` POSIX line-atomicity ≤ PIPE_BUF guarantee for JSONL appends; `O_EXCL` for first-write no-clobber).

.claude/rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:31:# Advanced agent team E2E unleashed — standing directive
.claude/rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:33:## The rule
.claude/rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:35:Every non-trivial fire (architecture audit / SOTA convergence / token-eff investigation / cross-model verification / gap-resolution wave) MUST spawn a 3-5 agent team. **Scope**: output fires only — rule-edit fires are governed by `cross-model-consensus.md` T1/T2/T3 gates alone, not this standing directive. **CADP cap**: concurrent dispatch is bounded by `parallel-agent-wave.md §CADP rule 2` (max 3 concurrent unless `python Z:/claude/ccc/tools/status.py` shows cache ≥50% verified); 4-5 agents MUST use staggered dispatch — see §Anti-patterns for the full CADP carve-out. The team MUST satisfy ALL of:
.claude/rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:37:1. **GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE for ≥2 agents** — `codex-rescue` / `gpt5-reviewer` / `gpt5-archaeologist` (Sonnet wrapper invoking real GPT-5.5 via codex CLI); satisfies cross-model-asymmetry per `cross-model-consensus.md`. **Per-call codex time-budget mandate** (Wave 44 FM-17.d codification, refined Wave 45 Fire 1 per codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.84 Pattern A): each codex call within agent brief MUST budget time per call -- **default 90s, normal cap 120s, 180s only with explicit reason**. Bounded, independently answerable calls preferred over one xhigh open-ended call when the question decomposes cleanly; keep one call when setup/context dominates OR the answer requires integrated judgment. Wrapper stream-watchdog 600s timeout fires before xhigh codex completion (empirical 5-15min per `cross-model-consensus.md §Profile selection rule`); short-call budget bypasses watchdog OR enables main-session foreground+tee fallback per `fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md §FM-17.d` recovery
.claude/rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:44:8. **OUTPUT_BUDGET and TERMINATION in every brief** — every agent brief MUST include `OUTPUT_BUDGET: <max LOC | max words>` (bounds artifact size for orchestrator ingestion per `team-orchestration.md §OUTPUT_BUDGET semantic`) AND `TERMINATION: on_handoff_to: orchestrator | max_turns: N | terminationCondition: <predicates>` per `team-orchestration.md §Termination contract`
.claude/rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:46:## Why
.claude/rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:48:- **n=3 user-trigger** ladder: 2026-05-04 (`feedback_always_launch_advanced_agent_team_2026_05_04.md`) + 2026-05-05 fire 1 + 2026-05-05 fire-Wave-24-D promotion (this rule); per `codification-threshold.md` cycle-322 jurisdiction § user-trigger n=1 automatic + n=2+ firms convention
.claude/rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:53:## How to apply
.claude/rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:100:- **BRIDGE-MODE GPT-5.5 brief without per-call codex time-budget mandate (default 90s / cap 120s / 180s with reason)** — produces FM-17.d wrapper stream-watchdog stall at 600s (n=3 firm cumulative same-arc 2026-05-06 Wave 41 Agent C INTERNAL_ERROR + Wave 43 Agents α/β both 600s stalls per `fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md §FM-17.d`). Recovery: foreground+tee from main session OR re-brief with explicit per-call budget
.claude/rules\advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md:102:- **Spawn beyond `parallel-agent-wave.md §Cache-Aware Dispatch Pacing` caps** — refuted by §CADP rule 2 (`max 3 concurrent` until cache rate ≥50% verified via `python Z:/claude/ccc/tools/status.py`) AND §CADP rule 4 (`max 5 cumulative dispatches per session arc without intervening status.py probe`). Earlier "3-5 safe band" framing in this directive (n=2 codification) was self-contradicting per Wave 23 Agent K P1 finding 2026-05-05 — `parallel-agent-wave.md:189-194` is authority; this directive defers. .claude/rules\named-failure-modes.md:42:| **FM-15 git CLI grammar option-after-`--` misordering** | Operator places dashed option (`-F`, `-m`, `--only`, etc.) AFTER `--` separator on `git <subcommand>`; git correctly interprets the option as pathspec per gitcli.adoc semantic; error `pathspec '<flag>' did not match any file(s) known to git`. Bug is operator-side ordering — git is correct | n=1 user-trigger 2026-05-03 ("why is your git practice is always wrong? find sota repos and gap resolute that"); trigger incident: `git commit --only -- .claude/settings.json -F tmp/ship2-commit-msg.txt` (Ship 2 commit attempt); promotion gate `codification-threshold.md` cycle-322 jurisdiction § user-trigger n=1 automatic | Place ALL options BEFORE `--` separator: `git commit --only -F tmp/msg.txt -- .claude/settings.json`. .claude/rules\parallel-agent-wave.md:162:## Anti-patterns
.claude/rules\parallel-agent-wave.md:166:- **Synthesis-before-completion** (qualified): for **bounded required-axis waves**, wait for every required axis before final synthesis. For **exploratory swarms**, declare quorum up front per `team-orchestration.md` (LOCAL — sister-port iter-#5) (80% threshold); synthesis may proceed at quorum but MUST mark missing axes as absent (not silently complete).
.claude/rules\parallel-agent-wave.md:168:- **Default cap exceeded without contract**: default research/audit wave size is 3-6. Use 7+ only with an explicit shard contract, non-overlapping scopes, budget cap, and synthesis plan; for 10-20 file migrations, use `team-orchestration.md` (LOCAL — sister-port iter-#5) or the swarm/migration recipe in `parallel-sessions.md` (LOCAL — ported iter-12). Empirical: `cycle-c0cd0eac` shows 7 can work with explicit Explore-agent contract; absent contract, 7+ produces redundant findings without proportional coverage gain.
.claude/rules\parallel-agent-wave.md:170:- **Recursive fan-out / child agents spawning grandchildren**: per `team-orchestration.md` (LOCAL — sister-port iter-#5) single-level fork invariant — children must NOT spawn further teammates.

.claude/rules\fm21-queue-time-prompt-freeze.md:19:## Origin & promotion
.claude/rules\fm21-queue-time-prompt-freeze.md:33:## Sub-classes (a/b) — taxonomy
.claude/rules\fm21-queue-time-prompt-freeze.md:37:| **a CronCreate freeze** (recurring) | `CronCreate({cron, prompt, recurring})` stores prompt verbatim; re-fires identically each tick until explicit CronDelete | Active cron loads stale prompt at every `*/N * * * *` boundary; `[INFERRED]` no CronUpdate primitive documented → must `CronDelete + CronCreate` to refresh | Operator-action `CronDelete <id> + CronCreate <same-cron-expr, v(N+1)-prompt>` at doctrine-evolution-ship boundary. Codify cron-prompt-update emit as MANDATORY ship-step in `audit-action-loop.md §Stage 4 Re-fire` Wire→Surface→Close discipline (defers to next compact + fresh `/loop` invocation if operator unavailable) |
.claude/rules\fm21-queue-time-prompt-freeze.md:40:## Sub-class detail (operator-runbook expansions)
.claude/rules\fm21-queue-time-prompt-freeze.md:44:CronCreate is the primary cron-mode `/loop` entry: prompt body captured at create time + replayed each tick. `[INFERRED from /scheduled-tasks docs]` no in-place prompt update mechanism documented (only CronCreate / CronList / CronDelete). Doctrine-evolution ships within an active cron arc will land at runtime BUT cron tick continues to load STALE v(N) prompt. Recovery is operator-side: at doctrine-evolution-ship boundary, emit a manifest entry queuing `CronDelete <job_id> + CronCreate <cron, v(N+1)-prompt>` for next operator interaction. Sister to `audit-action-loop.md §Stage 4 Re-fire` Wire→Surface→Close discipline.

.claude/rules\multi-source-discovery-breadth-discipline.md:72:## When NOT to apply
.claude/rules\multi-source-discovery-breadth-discipline.md:80:## Anti-patterns
.claude/rules\multi-source-discovery-breadth-discipline.md:82:- **GitHub-only search** — refuted by user-doc §Dimension A "the discovery layer must be polyglot"; produces bias toward TypeScript/JavaScript + recent + popular
.claude/rules\multi-source-discovery-breadth-discipline.md:88:- **Counting multiple endpoints from one provider as multiple distinct sources** (per Pattern A codex T1 Fire 29a prescription #6) — refuted by §Discovery cohort "Distinct source definition" + §Counting rules: a single provider (GitHub / DeepWiki / Exa / Context7 / repomix) collapses ALL its sub-capabilities into ONE source-family count. Example anti-pattern: querying `mcp__github__search_repositories` + `mcp__github__search_code` + `mcp__github__list_commits` + `gh CLI repo view` and claiming "4 sources" — actual count = 1 source (GitHub family). Must query ≥4 DIFFERENT provider families.
.claude/rules\multi-source-discovery-breadth-discipline.md:89:- **Counting INSTALLED-NOT-WIRED sources as available without verifying alternate path** (per Pattern A codex T1 Fire 29a prescription #4+#5) — refuted by §Counting rules: NOT-WIRED sources require documented working alternate path (WebFetch / HTTP-direct / ctx_fetch_and_index) AND relevant-results return. Example anti-pattern: Perplexity@0.8.4 npm-global is INSTALLED but NOT in current .mcp.json; cannot count as "MCP source" until either wired OR queried via WebFetch fallback with relevant results.

.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:16:# Synthesis-layer verify — git-diff before commit, decompose before flip
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:18:# TIER-3 port provenance only: ported from Z:/claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md HEAD. Parent CCC retains the n=9 dogfood chain and cycle-N evidence; it is not claude-sota design authority.
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:22:## Why
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:24:Ship-reports drift from git reality in BOTH directions OR fail to surface a finding entirely (HONEST-NON-FINDING — see §Reporting categories), and the failure-mode taxonomy keeps growing (agent flake, proxy restart, 429 retries, Windows loopback transients, model-side stalls, infrastructure deploys mid-cycle). The synthesis-layer recovery is the same single check across all of them: `git diff` + `git status` + smoke-import. Without this check, false-failure re-spawns waste 2-5 min each; false-success commits introduce silent no-ops into git history.
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:26:## How to apply (team-lead pre-commit checklist)
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:42:## SubagentStop transcript-mining (4th OVER/UNDER/HNF detection axis — iter-109 closure)
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:46:**Operational form**: `.claude/hooks/scripts/subagent_transcript_mine.py` SubagentStop hook (Layer 3 async fail-open observer per `layered-gates-architecture.md §6`) appends to `.claude/state/subagent_transcripts.jsonl` — schema: `{ts, agent_id, agent_type, transcript_path, last_text[:2000], tool_count, tool_errors, parse_status}`. Sentinel discipline per Karpathy P1 lenient-mode: `parse_status` ∈ {ok / no_transcript_path / transcript_missing / parse_empty / parse_failed}; `-1` for tool_count/tool_errors when uncertain.
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:50:- **UNDER (transcript surfaces unreported tool-errors)**: agent's report doesn't mention errors but transcript shows `tool_errors > 0` — agent encountered failures it suppressed. Recovery: surface the suppressed errors per cardinal-rule 7 (CLAUDE.md §Hard Rules, bullet "REPORT errors before routing around them"); pivot ship if errors load-bearing.
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:53:**Caveat (per `agent-harness-fit-verification.md` Probe 5 mode-harness-shape)**: hook fires on SubagentStop; if SDK runtime ceiling (e.g., max_turns) terminates the session before SubagentStop fires, this hook has no event to mine. Verdicts using this axis MUST mark `[VERIFIED via .claude/state/subagent_transcripts.jsonl]` per `cross-model-consensus.md §Evidence marker discipline` MUST-USE convention codified iter-107.
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:56:- `.claude/rules/audit-action-loop.md §Hook telemetry contract` — same `agent_id`/`agent_type` propagation contract; this hook follows the SDK schema at top-level
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:57:- `.claude/rules/parallel-agent-wave.md §Cache-Aware Dispatch Pacing` — `tool_count` mining feeds cost-aware termination predicate per iter-108 codification
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:58:- `.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md §Stuck Detection` — `tool_errors > 0` mining feeds 3-failed-attempts STOP signal
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:59:- `.claude/rules/cross-model-consensus.md §Evidence marker discipline` — `[VERIFIED via path]` MUST-USE per iter-107 codification applies to JSONL cite
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:61:## Reporting categories — OVER, UNDER, HONEST-NON-FINDING
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:75:## Subclaim-type discriminator (UPSTREAM / OPERATIONAL / CATEGORY)
.claude/rules\synthesis-layer-verify.md:92:**Distinguishes from §Reporting categories above**: §Reporting categories classifies REPORT shape (OVER/UNDER/HNF — about agent's output). §Subclaim-type discriminator classifies SOURCE shape (UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY — about what's being verified). Orthogonal axes — a UPSTREAM-CLAIM probe may produce HNF, OVER, or UNDER report.

.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:24:## §1 The four drift classes
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:33:## §2 Discipline 1 — Port-note line-offset compensation
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:54:## §3 Discipline 2 — FORWARD-REF retirement audit
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:56:Rules + port-notes routinely write `FORWARD-REF — <X> pending P<N> port` for things not yet shipped. When P<N> ships, every such cite must be rewritten to drop the FORWARD-REF qualifier (or escalate to a stronger status — but see §6 anti-pattern about `ACTIVE`).
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:66:2. For each match: rewrite to current status. **DO NOT escalate to ACTIVE without verifying the runtime wire** (see §6 anti-pattern).
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:70:## §4 Discipline 3 — Cite-class verbatim verify (recursive synthesis-layer-verify)
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:72:Per `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Reporting categories, codex T1 verdicts are themselves subject to OVER/UNDER/HONEST-NON-FINDING audit. Pattern P3 (inherited upstream cite errors) demonstrates the recursive application: if a cite is verbatim-ported from upstream BUT the upstream cite was wrong, the port preserves the error AND codex T1 (which checks file:line existence not content) doesn't catch it.
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:82:## §5 Discipline 4 — n-counter audit (claim-vs-actual reconciliation)
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:98:**Cycle-322 promotion-gate guards**: this rule's existence is itself an n=4-promoted gate (P1) + n=2-promoted gate (P2 + P4). Per discipline §5, those promotions are verified:
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:99:- P1 n=4: verified via Agent B audit per `agent-a46d7ce915a870480 retrospective verdict §1-§5` — utils.py port-note self-cite L47/L63 + _guard_base.py:6 cite to utils.py:107-134 + pyright_guard.py port-note "utils.py L66" + ruff.toml cite "utils.py:107" = 4 sites.
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:103:## §6 Anti-patterns
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:105:- **"Verbatim port preserves correctness"** — refuted by Pattern P3. Verbatim port preserves CONTENT but not CORRECTNESS-OF-CITES; inherited cite errors propagate forever otherwise. Cite-class verbatim verify (§4) is the recursive check.
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:106:- **"Cite-class-only audit (file existence)"** — refuted by Pattern P3. Codex T1 saw FORWARD-REF status without content verify. Add §4 discipline to T1 prompt template.
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:107:- **"Bump line numbers blindly"** — refuted by §2 Discipline 1 caveat. Line-bump compensation is INVALID when port edit involves any content addition/deletion beyond the port-note. Symbol-anchor is the safer SOTA pattern.
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:108:- **"FORWARD-REF retirement is hook-driven; ship it now"** — partially refuted by current `.claude/settings.json` state. Hook stack registers PreToolUse Edit|Write|MultiEdit for T1 and PostToolUse Bash(git commit *) for postcommit review/audits, but still lacks a dedicated T3 retirement-grep consumer [VERIFIED 2026-04-28 via `.claude/settings.json:273-335`]. T1 enforcement and generic postcommit review are hook-driven; FORWARD-REF retirement remains iter-end manual grep per §3 until a dedicated consumer lands.
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:109:- **"Script-landed = ACTIVE"** — CATEGORY-CLAIM drift per `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Subclaim-type discriminator. A hook script existing at `.claude/hooks/scripts/X.py` does NOT mean it fires at runtime. Verify wire status by reading `.claude/settings.json` BEFORE flipping any rule's lifecycle table to ACTIVE. Honest intermediate states: `script-landed/unwired`, `script-landed/wired-pending-codex-CLI-install`, `script-landed/wired/disabled-by-env`. Use them.
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:113:## §7 Sibling-rule integration
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:115:- `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` — §Reporting categories OVER/UNDER/HONEST-NON-FINDING applies recursively to codex T1 output (§4 Discipline 3). Subclaim-type discriminator UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY surfaces "script-landed = ACTIVE" as CATEGORY-CLAIM drift (§6 anti-pattern).
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:116:- `.claude/rules/cross-model-consensus.md` — §T1-T6 lifecycle table is ITSELF subject to §3 FORWARD-REF retirement discipline. Lifecycle status flips MUST verify wire-state per §6 anti-pattern.
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:117:- `.claude/rules/audit-action-loop.md` — §4-stage Wire / Surface / Close / Re-fire pattern is the mechanization analogue of §3 FORWARD-REF retirement. When FUTURE-AUTOMATION lands (postcommit hook wiring), this rule's §3 discipline becomes audit-action-loop's "Surface" stage.
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:118:- `.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md` — n-counter claims (§5) require `[VERIFIED via <query>]` markers per evidence-policy. Bare n-counter claims without query attribution fail the marker discipline.
.claude/rules\port-note-discipline.md:119:- `.claude/rules/sota-pin-discipline.md` — Tier-1/Tier-3 cited-repo HEAD SHAs in PINS.json are subject to §4 cite-class verbatim verify when the parenthetical claim about content drifts on upstream changes.

.claude/rules\layered-gates-architecture.md:299:## §13 Anti-patterns (what NOT to do)
.claude/rules\layered-gates-architecture.md:308:## §15 Honest limits of asyncRewake (codex T1 feedback codification)
.claude/rules\layered-gates-architecture.md:334:**Any future operator-side composition of upstream hook primitives that changes enforcement semantics MUST carry an "honest limits" caveat inline OR in its own section.** This applies whenever the composition: (a) converts an upstream primitive into a local safety policy, (b) uses detection-after-action semantics and might be mistaken for prevention, (c) makes fail-open/fail-closed prescriptions, (d) removes or weakens a prior enforcement layer (cf. §4.1 Wave 11A removal), OR (e) depends on local empirical evidence rather than upstream guarantee. The §15 asyncRewake section is the EXEMPLAR. Cross-ref: `citation-discipline.md` rule #8 composed-claims discipline (Wave 17 fire-31 codification)

.claude/rules\fm20-path-drift-cascade.md:64:## How to apply (forward-only convention)
.claude/rules\fm20-path-drift-cascade.md:68:1. **Decompose by sub-claim** — break the agent's "X+Y+Z" return into 3 independent claims; each gets its own probe shape per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` (UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY)
.claude/rules\fm20-path-drift-cascade.md:72:5. **At next-fire dispatch**: brief cites only verified sub-claims + explicit `[VERIFIED via <probe>]` markers per `cross-model-consensus.md §Evidence marker discipline` MUST-USE convention codified iter-107
.claude/rules\fm20-path-drift-cascade.md:74:## Recovery actions when FM-20 fires
.claude/rules\fm20-path-drift-cascade.md:80:3. **Pivot ship to verified-surviving prescriptions** OR HONEST-NON-FINDING if all sub-claims refuted (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories`)
.claude/rules\fm20-path-drift-cascade.md:82:5. **Forward-only correction** per `port-note-discipline.md §6` — do NOT rewrite historical commit bodies; correct the LIVE state and codify forward
.claude/rules\fm20-path-drift-cascade.md:84:## Anti-patterns
.claude/rules\fm20-path-drift-cascade.md:89:- **Treat HONEST-NON-FINDING as agent failure** — per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` HNF distinction. An agent that probes its own claim and self-refutes (Agent A fire-22 ccusage self-catch; Agent A Wave 37 reverse-Mia AGENTS.md+context7) is the highest-quality return shape — preserve as evidence, do not re-spawn.

.claude/rules\team-orchestration.md:580:| 3 | **No nested teams** | A teammate calling `TeamCreate` OR spawning additional teammates from inside its own session | Per §Parallel Spawning Rules "Single-level fork invariant": teammate returns to lead; lead decides whether to spawn a sibling. Recursive teammate-spawn — including direct child-spawning — is rejected by the runtime; design briefs MUST NOT instruct nested team or recursive teammate spawn |
.claude/rules\team-orchestration.md:590:| 8 | **Slow shutdown** | Calling `TeamDelete` cleanup immediately after `shutdown_request` SendMessage | Wait for current tool/MCP/Bash request in flight to finish AND for `shutdown_approved` SendMessage response BEFORE TeamDelete. Per §Team-Lifecycle Hygiene step 2: send shutdown_request → await shutdown_approved → THEN TeamDelete |

## Canonical ECC orchestration skills (prefer over hand-rolled)

ECC ships **3 production-tested orchestration skills** listed below; `claude-devfleet` is **reference-only** in sss until its DevFleet MCP backend is wired (see `## Reference-only ECC skills NOT wired in sss` subsection below). **Cite these before reinventing** — parent-cycle-132 caught a 0-cite gap:

| Skill | When to activate | Canonical for |
|-------|------------------|---------------|
| `dmux-workflows` | Multiple agent sessions in parallel (CC + Codex + OpenCode). User says "run in parallel", "split this work", "multi-agent". | **Parallel panes across harnesses**. 3 patterns: Research+Implement, Multi-File Feature, Test+Fix Loop. |
| `autonomous-agent-harness` | User wants continuous/scheduled operation, self-directing loops. Replaces Hermes/AutoGPT. | **Long-running loops** with Crons + Dispatch + Memory + Computer Use layers. /loop fires are a SUBSET. |
| `team-builder` | Need to compose ad-hoc team, browse available agents. | **Interactive picker** — uses `claude agents` CLI + filesystem glob fallback. |

 succeeded in 443ms:
not the primary source.

**STRUCTURAL vs OPERATIONAL**: structural constraints (#1-6) produce
"illegal-design-assumption" failures — the runtime rejects the operation OR
the action doesn't have the intended effect (e.g., resumed teammate is
silently absent). Operational limitations (#7-8) are runtime behaviors
that require active mitigation patterns; they do NOT produce hard-rejection,
they produce delayed-completion or stale-state. Match recovery to category.

`016b02f` (4-limitation initial draft) → iter-21 fix-forward (this version,
expanding to all 8 official limitations per codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION 0.88
prescriptions). Pattern A FIX-FORWARD per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md`
Outcome A — single iter close.

## Parent→Child State-Leak Avoidance (negative enumeration)

# Reference: Z:/repos/deps/deepagents/libs/deepagents/deepagents/middleware/subagents.py:164-176,384,409 @ HEAD 95f845d29745ece957144d045849f02c667ac711 [VERIFIED 2026-04-30 via Read full file + codex T1 ADOPT-NOW-DOC-ONLY conf=0.84] (LangChain deepagents SubAgentMiddleware `_EXCLUDED_STATE_KEYS` 5-key set + 12-line rationale at :164-176; inbound parent→child filtering at :384; outbound child→parent filtering at :409; license MIT)

The "Agents have ZERO parent context" mandate at §"Agent Brief Template" above is **positive enumeration only** — it specifies what TO include in briefs. This subsection adds the **negative enumeration**: what to EXPLICITLY EXCLUDE from parent→child Agent() spawn boundaries. The 5-key set converges with deepagents' SOTA SubAgentMiddleware pattern (LangChain-org-backed, MIT-licensed).

### The 5-key state-leak set (deepagents-aligned, sss-fit translated)

deepagents source: `_EXCLUDED_STATE_KEYS = {"messages", "todos", "structured_response", "skills_metadata", "memory_contents"}` (verbatim at subagents.py:164-176). Translated to sss concrete no-pass set per iter-25.4 g030 verdict prescription:

| sss no-pass key | Matches deepagents | Why excluded |
|---|---|---|
| `parent_brief_text` | `messages` (parent transcript / brief prompt) | Pasting parent's prompt verbatim into child brief breaks `coordination.md` §3 rule 1 independent-generation invariant; child gets focused brief only |
| `other_agents_outputs` | `messages` (sibling chains) | Pasting sibling parallel agents' outputs as `CONTEXT` breaks independent-generation per same rule; orchestrator synthesizes after both return |
| `telemetry_jsonl_path` | `todos` + `structured_response` (write-access state) | Don't grant write access to session-scoped JSONL trails; each agent appends to its own; parent merges |
| `upstream_state_dir` | `skills_metadata` (cache pointer) | Don't grant access to `.claude/state/<other-team>/` from a sibling team — cross-team C1 namespace collision per §"Session Isolation" |
| `parent_secrets_env` | `memory_contents` (env-resolved sensitive) | Don't propagate parent's resolved env per `security-checklist.md` secret-management; child gets env from its own context |

The mapping is **structural-similarity, not 1:1** — sss uses prompt-string handoff via `Agent({prompt})` where deepagents uses LangGraph typed-state `Command(update={...})`. Both share the negative-enumeration shape.

### Out-of-band channel list (minimum examples — not exhaustive)

Common leak channels in this repo:

- **Plain prompt/context leakage**: parent transcript, parent brief text, sibling agent outputs pasted into `CONTEXT` block of child brief
- **Bash `--env VAR=value`** arg propagation (env leaks credentials per memory `reference_userprofile_credential_collision.md`)
- **File-path references to** `.claude/state/<parent-task-id>/` (cross-task C1 collision per §"Session Isolation")
- **`.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota-installed/<session>/tool-results/*`** (session-scoped tool output; already cited as sss alternative at team-orchestration.md:74)
- **`.claude/projects/*/memory/MEMORY.md` or `feedback_*.md`/`reference_*.md`** as raw state (child should independently RECALL via `research-protocol.md`, not consume parent's recall results)
- **TodoWrite items pre-populated with parent's plan** (C1 namespace collision)
- **Shared `.claude/sessions/<id>/` paths** (TIER-3 provenance, not authority)
- **Inherited shell environment generally** (not just literal Bash `--env` arg; PATH / HOME / CLAUDE_* / OPENAI_* / ANTHROPIC_* env vars all carry potential leak)

### Bidirectional discipline (child → parent return path)

The same exclusion applies on the RETURN path. deepagents at `subagents.py:384` filters inbound (parent→child) and at `:409` filters outbound (child→parent). For sss:

- Child agents return via task-notification result; orchestrator MUST NOT trust returned state-keys against the same exclusion set without spot-verify
- Apply `synthesis-layer-verify.md` §"Reporting categories" (OVER/UNDER/HONEST-NON-FINDING) to every returned state-key
- See `feedback_keyword_vs_pattern_similarity_2026_04_30.md` for the recursive synthesis-layer-verify discipline that catches OVER-claims at agent-output layer

### Update triggers

Re-evaluate this rule when:
- A 6th state-key class emerges (deepagents adds it upstream OR eee-local incident reveals one)
- A 4th named-T2 agent SDK adopts equivalent negative-enumeration (current named-T2: deepagents + langchain core; openai-agents-python uses dataclass+Runner shape; claude-agent-sdk uses ClaudeAgentOptions)
- A workflow spawns **>5 concurrent teammates** (token budget threshold per team-orchestration.md:392) OR uses the **7+ shard-contract pattern** per `parallel-agent-wave.md:92` OR uses the **10-20 swarm/migration recipe** per `parallel-sessions.md:73,118` — concurrent-team thresholds drive state-leak risk; agent definition file count does not

### Provenance

- Wave 9 architecture-vs-SOTA audit, agent `af32addc` 2026-04-30 (gap #2 PRIORITY-1 BUNDLE)
- Prior codex T1 ADOPT-NOW-DOC-ONLY verdict conf=0.84 at `.claude/state/agent_team_iter25_4_g030_deepagents_OUT.txt:175-196` (audit prescribed; not previously shipped)
- Fresh codex T1 ADOPT-NOW-DOC-ONLY verdict conf=0.84 at `.claude/state/codex_consult_state_leak_avoidance_OUT.txt` 2026-04-30 (5 fix-forward prescriptions applied per `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md` Pattern A: license `MIT` not `Apache-2.0`; cite range `:164-176,384,409`; g030 concrete sss set; expanded out-of-band channel list; concurrent-team threshold)

## Implementer status vocabulary (4-state DONE / DONE_WITH_CONCERNS / NEEDS_CONTEXT / BLOCKED)

# Reference: Z:/repos/deps/superpowers/skills/subagent-driven-development/SKILL.md:103-127 @ HEAD e7a2d16476bf042e9add4699c9d018a90f86e4a6 [VERIFIED 2026-04-30 via wave-3 source-mining methodology-audit-PASS at docs/sota-grand-catalog/wave-3-2026-04-30/source-mining/superpowers-deep-read.md (commit 11bf5eb)]

Per superpowers (named-T2 author obra; 3-axis convergence-gate PASS), **implementer subagents** (those that write code, not those that review) MUST report one of four canonical statuses in their `verdict_one_line` HANDOFF slot. This vocabulary disambiguates "completed but uncertain" from "completed clean" and enables team-lead to route follow-up correctly without re-prompting the implementer:

 succeeded in 403ms:
- §"Snapshot-labeling + recovery-pointer discipline" below — companion: when arg is truncated, the placeholder MUST name the recovery tool/path (per Anthropic CC convention)
- `reference_loop7h_2026_04_29_grand_catalog.md` + `reference_loop_7h_synthesis_2026_04_28.md` — empirical anchors for long-arc tool/verdict volume, repeated Edit/Write activity, and context-pressure conditions that motivate pre-emptive arg truncation discipline

**Snapshot-labeling + recovery-pointer discipline [SSS-local]**: when a tool output is truncated under budget, the emitter MUST label the snapshot (`[truncated at N tokens]`) AND name the recovery tool to fetch the full output (`call X with id Y for complete output`). Silent truncation forces the model to infer absence vs limit. Applies to `.claude/hooks/scripts/` printing to stderr under sys.exit(2) rewake and to Agent tool results exceeding `MAX_AGENT_OUTPUT`. (Prior cite to `awesome-agentic-patterns/patterns/context-window-auto-compaction.md:1` removed per codex T1 G-024 F-003 — that pattern covers context overflow auto-compaction, not truncated-output recovery pointers; the snapshot-labeling rule is sss-internal discipline.)

**Context-memoization invalidation gotcha [SSS-local]**: progressive-disclosure rule files + component CLAUDE.md are memoized per session. Adding a new context source (`.claude/rules/foo.md`, `.claude/agents/bar.md`, skill) WITHOUT updating its referring index leaves the model with a stale view for the rest of the session. (Prior cite to `context-minimization-pattern.md:1` removed per codex T1 G-024 F-002 — upstream pattern at `:13-23` is about purging tainted user/tool-output text after transformation into trusted intermediate artifacts, not session memoization invalidation.)

**Context-minimization discipline**: after untrusted user text or raw tool output has been transformed into a trusted intermediate, purge or redact the original tainted segment; downstream agents should consume only signed-off structured artifacts. Ref: `Z:/repos/deps/awesome-agentic-patterns/patterns/context-minimization-pattern.md:13-23 @ HEAD ffb427683ec77f3690f7fadfec7a7611d9e907d9 [VERIFIED 2026-04-28]`.

## Agent Brief Template

Agents have ZERO parent context — briefs must be fully self-contained:

**Capability discovery**: when a brief needs a tool/API not already pinned in the environment, prefer agent-ready discovery with structured metadata, protocol-native access, and active verification over human SEO/manual docs. Ref: `Z:/repos/deps/awesome-agentic-patterns/patterns/agent-first-tool-discovery.md:15-29,51-54 @ HEAD ffb427683ec77f3690f7fadfec7a7611d9e907d9 [VERIFIED 2026-04-28]` (NEW pattern added 2026-04-22 per upstream commit log; G-024 fire 1 cite refresh).

```
Agent({
  subagent_type: "<agent-name>",
  prompt: "
    TASK: <what to do — specific, measurable>
    CONTEXT: <all necessary background — don't assume knowledge>
    FILES: <exact paths to read/modify>
    CONSTRAINTS: <limits, patterns to follow>
    OUTPUT: <expected format and where to write it>
    OUTPUT_BUDGET: <max LOC | max words | max artifact tokens for the returned written artifact>
    DONE WHEN: <measurable completion criteria>
    HANDOFF: handoff_to: team-lead, output_mode: last_message, artifacts: [...], verdict_one_line: <X>
    TERMINATION: on_handoff_to: team-lead | max_turns: 12 | terminationCondition: <predicate-list>
  ",
  description: "<3-5 word summary>"
})
```

**HANDOFF** and **TERMINATION** slots added parent-cycle-154 iter-3 per LangGraph `Command(goto, graph=PARENT)` + AutoGen `TerminationCondition` T1 ports. Schema rationale at parent `templates/agent-brief.md` (PARENT-ATTRIBUTION — sss has no `templates/` dir; mirror pending if multi-agent orchestration grows in sss).

### OUTPUT_BUDGET semantic — Wave 20 codification per v4-frontier blueprint

# Reference: TIER-2 user-curated `Z:/claude-sota-installed/docs/_archives/kits-extracted-2026-05-05/v4-frontier/AUTOMATION_HARNESS_BLUEPRINT.md:30-40 [VERIFIED 2026-05-04 via direct Read; path updated 2026-05-05 Wave 19 fire-26 M2 mitigation]` — v4-frontier worker contract enumerates "maximum output budget" as a separate field

`OUTPUT_BUDGET: <max LOC | max words | max artifact tokens for the returned written artifact>` bounds the SIZE of the agent's report (the tmp/wave*.md content / ARTIFACT-INLINE return body / final-message text).

**Distinct from `terminationCondition: on_token_budget_exceeded:`** (cumulative session-budget telemetry evaluated post-completion from `agent_transcript_path` JSONL or ccusage CLI per §326 above; NOT artifact-length contract).

- **OUTPUT_BUDGET** = artifact-length contract: bounds report length so synthesis agent can ingest without re-summarization. Wave 20 example: gpt5-reviewer / sota-researcher / architect / gpt5-archaeologist briefs informally said "max 600 LOC" / "200-word summary" — this slot codifies the field that was already being used.
- **terminationCondition: on_token_budget_exceeded** = cumulative session-budget cap (post-completion); NOT a substitute for OUTPUT_BUDGET, NOT a runtime stop condition.

Use OUTPUT_BUDGET for synthesis-ingestion bounds; use `terminationCondition: on_token_budget_exceeded:` per `parallel-agent-wave.md §Cache-Aware Dispatch Pacing` for cost-aware termination.

### Termination contract (declarative predicates) — Wave 8 Agent B Gap #3 closure iter-108

Beyond the simple `max_turns: N` cap, the optional `terminationCondition:` slot accepts a **declarative composable predicate list** convergent with LangGraph `Command(goto, graph=PARENT)` + AutoGen `TerminationCondition` framework primitives. **Harness-fit Probe 5 mode-harness-shape** per `agent-harness-fit-verification.md` Probe 5: claude-sota is autonomous /loop mode + 2-model pair; the predicates are CC-Agent-tool-mode-compatible (post-completion notification predicates, NOT in-flight SDK polling).

**Supported predicates** (composable via `|` separator — first-match-wins; max_turns is the implicit ceiling):

| Predicate | Semantic | Example | Use case |
|---|---|---|---|
| `on_handoff_to: <agent>` | Agent emits `handoff_to: <agent>` in last message | `on_handoff_to: team-lead` | Standard subagent → orchestrator return (current default) |
| `on_text_match: "<literal>"` | Last assistant text contains literal substring (eee-local LITERAL semantic; AutoGen `TextMentionTermination` is literal, not regex) | `on_text_match: "VERDICT: APPROVE"` | Self-terminate on success-signal |
| `on_max_iterations: N` | Implicit max_turns cap (tool-call count proxy) | `on_max_iterations: 30` | Hard ceiling for runaway tasks |
| `on_token_budget_exceeded: <threshold>` | Cumulative token usage exceeds threshold | `on_token_budget_exceeded: 200000` | Cost-aware termination per `parallel-agent-wave.md §Cache-Aware Dispatch Pacing` |
| `on_tool_count_exceeded: N` | Tool-call count exceeds threshold | `on_tool_count_exceeded: 35` | Operator-observed safe-cap per `parallel-agent-wave.md §Cache-Aware Dispatch Pacing` |
| `on_subprocess_failure: <count>` | Repeated subprocess failures (Bash exit≠0) | `on_subprocess_failure: 3` | Stuck-detection per `evidence-policy.md §Stuck Detection` |

**Convergence cites** (TIER-1) — supported predicate subset, NOT the full AutoGen vocabulary (which also exposes `TimeoutTermination` / `StopMessageTermination` / `SourceMatchTermination` / `TextMessageTermination` / `FunctionCallTermination` / `FunctionalTermination`; sss adopts the 6-predicate subset above as the brief-template tier):
- LangGraph `Command(goto=..., graph=Command.PARENT)`: `https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api#command` [VERIFIED 2026-05-02 via codex T1 Q5 web-fetch — supersedes stale `langchain-ai.github.io/.../multi_agent/#command-and-graph-end` redirect]
- AutoGen concrete `*Termination` condition classes: `https://microsoft.github.io/autogen/stable/reference/python/autogen_agentchat.conditions.html` [VERIFIED 2026-05-02] — `TextMentionTermination` (literal not regex) / `MaxMessageTermination` / `TokenUsageTermination` / `ExternalTermination` / `HandoffTermination` etc.
- AutoGen `TerminationCondition` base class + composition operators: `https://microsoft.github.io/autogen/stable/reference/python/autogen_agentchat.base.html#autogen_agentchat.base.TerminationCondition` [VERIFIED 2026-05-02] — `|` (OR) and `&` (AND) composition. **eee-local subset**: adopts `|`-only first-match-wins at brief-template tier (avoids `&` AND-composition complexity); `first-match-wins` is an eee-local semantic, NOT inherited from AutoGen.

**Composition rule**: predicates evaluated post-completion-notification (CC Agent tool returns to orchestrator on natural completion OR exit signal); first matching predicate wins. `max_turns: N` is the harness/runtime ceiling NOT a SubagentStop predicate — when SDK `max_turns` fires, `SubagentStop` hook may NOT trigger, so terminationCondition predicates depending on SubagentStop won't evaluate at that boundary [VERIFIED 2026-05-02 via codex T1 Q4 caveat at .claude/state/codex_consult_iter108_termination_condition_slot_OUT.txt:1657]. Probe 5 mode-harness-shape PASS only for post-completion / post-fact evaluation: `on_token_budget_exceeded` and `on_tool_count_exceeded` are evaluated from `agent_transcript_path` JSONL or ccusage CLI output AFTER completion, NOT from direct SubagentStop payload fields (which expose only `agent_id` / `agent_type` / `agent_transcript_path` / `last_assistant_message`) or in-flight SDK polling.

**Backward compatibility**: briefs WITHOUT `terminationCondition:` (current default) continue to work — `on_handoff_to: team-lead | max_turns: N` is the implicit terminationCondition. The new slot is OPTIONAL; recommended only when multi-predicate termination is needed (cost-aware caps + success-pattern self-terminate + stuck-detection).

**Harness-fit gate** per `agent-harness-fit-verification.md`: Probe 5 mode-harness-shape PASS — predicates are post-completion (CC-Agent-tool-compatible, not SDK-mode-only). Probe 2 SDK-vs-CLI PASS — predicate evaluation can use existing CC notification mechanism + JSONL audit trail at `.claude/state/`. Probe 4 plugin-namespace PASS — no plugin owns terminationCondition.

**Cite anchor**: Wave 8 / Team #4 Agent B (architect) Gap #3 finding "graph-END/recursion-cap termination contract" at iter-103 dispatch task `ae38696701cc1e5a0` result; F-C-04 / F-C-03 (Agent C) NOT applicable here (this is Agent B's recommendation, not Agent C's).

## Parent→Child State-Leak Avoidance (negative enumeration)

# Reference: Z:/repos/deps/deepagents/libs/deepagents/deepagents/middleware/subagents.py:164-176,384,409 @ HEAD 95f845d29745ece957144d045849f02c667ac711 [VERIFIED 2026-04-30 via Read full file + codex T1 ADOPT-NOW-DOC-ONLY conf=0.84] (LangChain deepagents SubAgentMiddleware `_EXCLUDED_STATE_KEYS` 5-key set + 12-line rationale at :164-176; inbound parent→child filtering at :384; outbound child→parent filtering at :409; license MIT)

The "Agents have ZERO parent context" mandate at §"Agent Brief Template" above is **positive enumeration only** — it specifies what TO include in briefs. This subsection adds the **negative enumeration**: what to EXPLICITLY EXCLUDE from parent→child Agent() spawn boundaries. The 5-key set converges with deepagents' SOTA SubAgentMiddleware pattern (LangChain-org-backed, MIT-licensed).

### The 5-key state-leak set (deepagents-aligned, sss-fit translated)

deepagents source: `_EXCLUDED_STATE_KEYS = {"messages", "todos", "structured_response", "skills_metadata", "memory_contents"}` (verbatim at subagents.py:164-176). Translated to sss concrete no-pass set per iter-25.4 g030 verdict prescription:

| sss no-pass key | Matches deepagents | Why excluded |
|---|---|---|

 succeeded in 428ms:

Decompose complex tasks into dependency waves:
```
Wave 1: [research-A, research-B]    ← parallel (spawn in single message)
Wave 2: [design]                     ← depends on wave 1
Wave 3: [impl-A, impl-B]            ← parallel
Wave 4: [verify]                     ← depends on wave 3
```

## 7 Orchestration Patterns

| Pattern | Use When | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Pipeline | Sequential A → B → C | /research → /plan → implement |
| Map-Reduce | Fan-out analysis, fan-in synthesis | 3 scouts → 1 synthesizer |
| Supervisor | Planning agent decomposes + assigns | team-lead → specialists |
| Swarm | Broad parallel exploration | N research scouts |
| Generator-Critic | Quality-critical write + review | implementation → code-reviewer |
| Debate | Two perspectives → judge selects | architect-A vs architect-B |
| Iterative Refinement | Draft → review → revise loop | implementation ↔ verifier |

For the fast-path decision gate (when to fan out 3-6 agents in parallel vs serial chain), see `parallel-agent-wave.md`. For same-artifact multi-lens review, see `multi-perspective-subagents.md`.

## Context Budget (per agent)

| Priority | Allocation | Content |
|---|---|---|
| Critical | 40% | Modified files, active errors, task requirements |
| Important | 30% | Related files, types, tests |
| Reference | 20% | Architecture docs, API specs |
